This module provides a pluggable data-quality validation engine that can be wired\n//! directly into Kafka consumer loops.\n//! It is designed to run in the ChirpPulse real-time stream processing services.\n\nuse std::{\n    collections::{HashMap, HashSet},\n    sync::Arc,\n    time::Duration,\n};\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse humantime::format_duration;\nuse log::{debug, error, info, warn};\nuse metrics::{counter, gauge, histogram};\nuse once_cell::sync::Lazy;\nuse regex::Regex;\nuse serde_json::Value;\nuse thiserror::Error;\nuse tokio::{\n    select,\n    sync::{mpsc, Semaphore},\n    task::JoinHandle,\n    time::{interval, sleep, Instant},\n};\n\n/// Maximum number of concurrent validation jobs.\n/// (safeguard to protect service from spikes)\nconst MAX_CONCURRENCY: usize = 256;\n\n/// Interval at which internal health metrics are flushed.\nconst METRIC_FLUSH_INTERVAL: Duration = Duration::from_secs(30);\n\n/// Wrapper around serde_json::Value that represents an incoming social event.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct SocialRecord {\n    pub raw: Value,\n    pub received_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n    /// Topic name (twitter, reddit, etc.)\n    pub source_topic: String,\n    /// Kafka offset (or any position marker)\n    pub offset: i64,\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Error)]\npub enum QualityError {\n    #[error(\"required field `{0}` missing\")]\n    MissingField(String),\n    #[error(\"field `{0}` failed regex validation\")]\n    RegexMismatch(String),\n    #[error(\"language detection mismatch: expected {expected}, got {actual}\")]\n    LanguageMismatch { expected: String, actual: String },\n    #[error(\"custom engine error: {0}\")]\n    Custom(String),\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\npub trait DataQualityCheck: Send + Sync {\n    /// Name of the check (used for logging & metrics).\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str;\n\n    /// Validate the provided record.\n    async fn validate(&self, record: &SocialRecord) -> Result<(), QualityError>;\n}\n\n/// Data quality check that asserts the presence of a non-empty field.\npub struct RequiredFieldCheck {\n    field: &'static str,\n}\nimpl RequiredFieldCheck {\n    pub fn new(field: &'static str) -> Self {\n        Self { field }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl DataQualityCheck for RequiredFieldCheck {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"required_field\"\n    }\n\n    async fn validate(&self, record: &SocialRecord) -> Result<(), QualityError> {\n        match record.raw.get(self.field) {\n            Some(v) if !v.is_null() => Ok(()),\n            _ => Err(QualityError::MissingField(self.field.into())),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Data quality check that applies a regex to a string field.\npub struct RegexFieldCheck {\n    field: &'static str,\n    regex: &'static Regex,\n}\n\nimpl RegexFieldCheck {\n    pub const fn new(field: &'static str, regex: &'static Regex) -> Self {\n        Self { field, regex }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl DataQualityCheck for RegexFieldCheck {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"regex_field\"\n    }\n\n    async fn validate(&self, record: &SocialRecord) -> Result<(), QualityError> {\n        match record.raw.get(self.field) {\n            Some(Value::String(s)) if self.regex.is_match(s) => Ok(()),\n            _ => Err(QualityError::RegexMismatch(self.field.into())),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Central engine that orchestrates execution of data-quality checks for incoming records.\npub struct QualityGate {\n    checks: Vec<Arc<dyn DataQualityCheck>>,\n    semaphore: Arc<Semaphore>,\n    reporter: mpsc::UnboundedSender<QualityReport>,\n}\n\nimpl QualityGate {\n    /// Build a new QualityGate with the provided checks.\n    pub fn new(checks: Vec<Arc<dyn DataQualityCheck>>) -> Self {\n        let (tx, rx) = mpsc::unbounded_channel();\n        let gate = Self {\n            checks,\n            semaphore: Arc::new(Semaphore::new(MAX_CONCURRENCY)),\n            reporter: tx,\n        };\n        gate.spawn_report_collector(rx);\n        gate\n    }\n\n    /// Validates a batch of social records concurrently. Returns handles that can be awaited\n    /// for completion.\n    pub fn validate_batch(&self, batch: Vec<SocialRecord>) -> Vec<JoinHandle<()>> {\n        let mut handles = Vec::with_capacity(batch.len());\n\n        for record in batch {\n            let permit = match self.semaphore.clone().try_acquire_owned() {\n                Ok(p) => p,\n                Err(_) => {\n                    // Should be exceedingly rare, but ensures we never panic.\n                    warn!(\"QualityGate semaphore at capacity; stalling new validations\");\n                    // Acquire asynchronously to wait.\n                    let semaphore = self.semaphore.clone();\n                    handles.push(tokio::spawn(async move {\n                        let _permit = semaphore.acquire().await;\n                        drop(_permit);\n                    }));\n                    continue;\n                }\n            };\n\n            let checks = self.checks.clone();\n            let tx = self.reporter.clone();\n\n            handles.push(tokio::spawn(async move {\n                let _alive_permit = permit; // keep permit alive for duration\n                let mut issues = Vec::new();\n\n                for check in &checks {\n                    match check.validate(&record).await {\n                        Ok(()) => {\n                            counter!(\n                                \"chirppulse_quality_check_pass_total\",\n                                1,\n                                \"check\" => check.name()\n                            );\n                        }\n                        Err(err) => {\n                            counter!(\n                                \"chirppulse_quality_check_fail_total\",\n                                1,\n                                \"check\" => check.name(),\n                                \"source_topic\" => record.source_topic.as_str()\n                            );\n                            issues.push(err);\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n\n                let report = QualityReport {\n                    record_meta: RecordMeta {\n                        source_topic: record.source_topic,\n                        offset: record.offset,\n                        received_at: record.received_at,\n                    },\n                    issues,\n                };\n\n                if let Err(e) = tx.send(report) {\n                    error!(\"quality report channel closed unexpectedly: {e}\");\n                }\n            }));\n        }\n\n        handles\n    }\n\n    /// Background task that aggregates and dumps quality reports.\n    fn spawn_report_collector(&self, mut rx: mpsc::UnboundedReceiver<QualityReport>) {\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            let mut buffer: Vec<QualityReport> = Vec::with_capacity(10_000);\n            let mut ticker = interval(METRIC_FLUSH_INTERVAL);\n\n            loop {\n                select! {\n                    maybe_report = rx.recv() => {\n                        match maybe_report {\n                            Some(report) => buffer.push(report),\n                            None => {\n                                debug!(\"QualityGate report channel closed; terminating collector\");\n                                break;\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n                    _ = ticker.tick() => {\n                        if !buffer.is_empty() {\n                            Self::flush_reports(std::mem::take(&mut buffer));\n                        }\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n\n            // final flush\n            if !buffer.is_empty() {\n                Self::flush_reports(buffer);\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    fn flush_reports(batch: Vec<QualityReport>) {\n        let total_issues: usize = batch.iter().map(|r| r.issues.len()).sum();\n        gauge!(\"chirppulse_quality_pending_reports\", 0.0);\n        histogram!(\"chirppulse_quality_issues_per_batch\", total_issues as f64);\n\n        if total_issues > 0 {\n            info!(\n                \"QualityGate flushed {} problematic records containing {} total issues\",\n                batch.len(),\n                total_issues\n            );\n            // In production this would be persisted to a DLQ, external store, or alerting bus.\n            for report in &batch {\n                for issue in &report.issues {\n                    warn!(\n                        \"[DQ] topic={} offset={} issue={}\",\n                        report.record_meta.source_topic, report.record_meta.offset, issue\n                    );\n                }\n            }\n        } else {\n            debug!(\n                \"QualityGate flushed {} clean records (no issues)\",\n                batch.len()\n            );\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Metadata we carry around for observability purposes.\n/// Kept separate from the `SocialRecord` to avoid cloning the full JSON document.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct RecordMeta {\n    pub source_topic: String,\n    pub offset: i64,\n    pub received_at: DateTime<Utc>,\n}\n\n/// A single validation report for a social record.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub struct QualityReport {\n    pub record_meta: RecordMeta,\n    pub issues: Vec<QualityError>,\n}\n\n/// A small helper that encapsulates an exponential back-off policy.\n///\n/// This is useful when interacting with external systems (e.g. Kafka, S3)\n/// where transient failures are expected during high traffic peaks.\npub struct Backoff {\n    next_delay: Duration,\n    max_delay: Duration,\n}\n\nimpl Backoff {\n    pub const fn new(initial: Duration, max_delay: Duration) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            next_delay: initial,\n            max_delay,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Wait for the current backoff delay, then exponentially increase it.\n    pub async fn snooze(&mut self) {\n        let wait = self.next_delay.min(self.max_delay);\n        debug!(\"backoff sleeping for {}\", format_duration(wait));\n        sleep(wait).await;\n        self.next_delay = (self.next_delay * 2).min(self.max_delay);\n    }\n\n    /// Reset the backoff delay to initial value.\n    pub fn reset(&mut self, initial: Duration) {\n        self.next_delay = initial;\n    }\n}\n\n/// A simple health-check watchdog that notifies if consumer lag grows beyond thresholds.\npub struct LagMonitor {\n    window: Duration,\n    critical_threshold: u64,\n    last_offsets: HashMap<String, i64>,\n    last_check: Instant,\n}\n\nimpl LagMonitor {\n    pub fn new(window: Duration, critical_threshold: u64) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            window,\n            critical_threshold,\n            last_offsets: HashMap::new(),\n            last_check: Instant::now(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Update the latest committed offset for the given topic.\n    pub fn record_offset(&mut self, topic: &str, offset: i64) {\n        self.last_offsets.insert(topic.to_owned(), offset);\n    }\n\n    /// Returns true if lag is considered healthy.\n    pub fn is_healthy(&mut self, fetch_lag: impl Fn(&str) -> Option<u64>) -> bool {\n        if self.last_check.elapsed() < self.window {\n            return true;\n        }\n        self.last_check = Instant::now();\n\n        for (topic, offset) in &self.last_offsets {\n            if let Some(lag) = fetch_lag(topic) {\n                gauge!(\"chirppulse_consumer_lag\", lag as f64, \"topic\" => topic.clone());\n\n                if lag > self.critical_threshold {\n                    error!(\n                        \"LagMonitor detected critical lag on topic `{}` ({} messages behind)\",\n                        topic, lag\n                    );\n                    return false;\n                } else {\n                    debug!(\n                        \"LagMonitor topic `{}` healthy (lag = {})\",\n                        topic, lag\n                    );\n                }\n            } else {\n                warn!(\n                    \"LagMonitor unable to fetch lag for topic `{}`; assuming unhealthy\",\n                    topic\n                );\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        true\n    }\n}\n\n// Pre-compiled regular expressions to prevent recompilation at runtime.\nstatic MENTION_RE: Lazy<Regex> = Lazy::new(|| Regex::new(r\"^@\\w{1,15}$\").unwrap());\n\n/// Example of how to wire        raw: json!({\"text\": \"hello world\"}),\n            received_at: Utc::now(),\n            source_topic: \"twitter_dev\".into(),\n            offset: 42,\n        };\n\n        let record_bad Module 18 – Schema Guard & Quality Gate\n//! ---------------------------------------\n//! This module provides _executable data-quality checks_ that are applied to\n//! every message that flows through ChirpPulse’s real-time pipelines.  The main\n//! responsibility is to guarantee that records entering the lake respect the\n//! agreed-upon contracts (schemas) while allowing **automatic forward /\n!   backward compatibility** through online schema evolution.\n//!\n//! High-level overview\n//! -------------------\n//! 1. Pull the latest registered JSONSchema/Avro schema for a _subject_ from\n//!    the central Schema Registry (Confluent-compatible API).\n//! 2. Compile and cache the schema in memory, tracking ETags + TTL.\n//! 3. Validate incoming records (raw bytes or JSON) in a streaming fashion.\n//! 4. Emit structured diagnostics and route poison-pill messages to a\n//!    quarantine topic for further inspection.\n//!\n//! The entire module is asynchronous, thread-safe, and ready to be injected\n//! into the Tokio-based streaming runtimes used across other micro-services.\n//!\n//! tokio           = { version = \"1.34\", features = [\"rt\", \"fs\", \"macros\"] }\n// serde           = { version = \"1.0\",  features = [\"derive\"] }\n// serde_json      = \"1.0\"\n// toml            = \"0.7\"\n// async-trait     = \"0.1\"\n// anyhow          = \"1.0\"\n// notify          = \"6.1\"\n// tracing         = \"0.1\"\n//\n// Optional (behind `kafka` feature):\n// rdkafka         = { Instant},\n};\n\nuse anyhow::{Context, Result};\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse notify::{Config as NotifyConfig, RecursiveMode, RecommendedWatcher, Watcher};\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\nuse tokio::{\n    fs,\n    select,\n    sync::{mpsc, watch, RwLock},\n    task,\n};\nuse tracing::{debug, error, info, warn};\n\n/// Maximum number of inflight events the channel will buffer before back-pressuring\nconst CHANNEL_CAPACITY: usize = 16_384;\n\n/// Public API surface\npub mod api {\n    use super::*;\n\n    /// Raw, unprocessed social-stream event\n    #[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n    pub struct RawEvent {\n        pub id: String,\n        pub user_id: String,\n        pub text: String,\n        pub timestamp: i64,\n        // Additional metadata elided\n    }\n\n    /// Enriched event emitted by the sentiment engine\n    #[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n    pub struct EnrichedEvent {\n        pub id: String,\n        pub user_id: String,\n        pub text: String,\n        pub timestamp: i64,\n        pub sentiment: f32,\n        pub processed_at: i64,\n        pub strategy_version: String,\n    }\n}\n\n// --------------- Strategy Pattern Implementation -----------------\n\n#[async_trait]\npub trait SentimentStrategy: Send + Sync {\n    /// Human-readable identifier (used for telemetry & auditing)\n    fn version(&self) -> &str;\n\n    /// Perform the analysis.  Must be fast (<1 ms) or spawn its own blocking task.\n    async fn score(&self, text: &str) -> Result<f32>;\n}\n\n/// Extremely naive, rule-based heuristic sentiment scorer.\n/// Suitable as a safe-mode fallback when heavy ML models fail.\npub struct RuleBasedStrategy;\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl SentimentStrategy for RuleBasedStrategy {\n    fn version(&self) -> &str {\n        \"rule-based-v1\"\n    }\n\n    async fn score(&self, text: &str) -> Result<f32> {\n        let lowered = text.to_ascii_lowercase();\n        let pos = [\"good\", \"love\", \"awesome\", \"yay\"]\n            .iter()\n            .filter(|w| lowered.contains(*w))\n            .count() as f32;\n        let neg = [\"bad\", \"hate\", \"terrible\", \"boo\"]\n            .iter()\n            .filter(|w| lowered.contains(*w))\n            .count() as f32;\n\n        Ok(match (pos - neg).clamp(-5.0, 5.0) {\n            x if x > 0.0 => x / 5.0,\n            x if x < 0.0 => x / 5.0,\n            _ => 0.0,\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n/// Placeholder for an ML-based implementation.  Here we fake the latency but\n/// the interface would realistically make a gRPC/tch-rs/onnxruntime call.\npub struct MlStrategy;\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl SentimentStrategy for MlStrategy {\n    fn version(&self) -> &str {\n        \"ml-bert-v3\"\n    }\n\n    async fn score(&self, text: &str) -> Result<f32> {\n        // Simulate inferencing latency\n        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(2)).await;\n        // For brevity: randomish score based on hash\n        let hash = seahash::hash(text.as_bytes());\n        Ok((hash % 2000) as f32 / 1000.0 - 1.0) // Value in [-1.0, 1.0]\n    }\n}\n\n// --------------- Config & Hot-Reloading ---------------------------\n\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(rename_all = \"snake_case\")]\nenum StrategyKind {\n    RuleBased,\n    Ml,\n}\n\nimpl StrategyKind {\n    fn build(self) -> Arc<dyn SentimentStrategy> {\n        match self {\n            StrategyKind::RuleBased => Arc::new(RuleBasedStrategy),\n            StrategyKind::Ml => Arc::new(MlStrategy),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]\npub struct EngineConfig {\n    /// Which strategy to use for scoring\n    strategy: StrategyKind,\n\n    /// How many enrichment worker tasks to spawn\n    #[serde(default = \"EngineConfig::default_parallelism\")]\n    parallelism: usize,\n}\n\nimpl EngineConfig {\n    fn default_parallelism() -> usize {\n        num_cpus::get().max(2)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl Default for EngineConfig {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            strategy: StrategyKind::RuleBased,\n            parallelism: Self::default_parallelism(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nasync fn load_config(path: &Path) -> Result<EngineConfig> {\n    let data = fs::read_to_string(path)\n        .await\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"reading config file {}\", path.display()))?;\n    let cfg: EngineConfig =\n        toml::from_str(&data).with_context(|| \"parsing engine config TOML\")?;\n    Ok(cfg)\n}\n\n// --------------- Sentiment Engine --------------------------------\n\npub struct SentimentEngine {\n    /// Sender for raw events\n    pub in_tx: mpsc::Sender<api::RawEvent>,\n    /// Receiver for enriched events\n    pub out_rx: mpsc::Receiver<api::EnrichedEvent>,\n    /// Handle to shutdown signal\n    shutdown_tx: watch::Sender<bool>,\n}\n","numLines":200,"startLine":1,"totalLines":451}}}

Critical Bottleneck: The mpsc Channel Coupling\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_27.txt\n// Lines: 44-47 (Channel depth constant)\n/// Maximum time a strategy may take before the orchestrator aborts it.\nconst STRATEGY_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_millis(800);\n\n/// Bounded size for the internal event queue.\nconst CHANNEL_DEPTH: usize = 1_024;\n```\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_27.txt\n// Lines: 209-228 (The critical bottleneck - bounded mpsc channels)\n    pub fn build(\n        self,\n    ) -> (\n        EnrichmentOrchestrator,\n        mpsc::Sender<SocialEvent>,\n        mpsc::Receiver<EnrichedEvent>,\n    ) {\n        let (in_tx, in_rx) = mpsc::channel(CHANNEL_DEPTH);\n        let (out_tx, out_rx) = mpsc::channel(CHANNEL_DEPTH);\n        (\n            EnrichmentOrchestrator {\n                input_rx: in_rx,\n                output_tx: out_tx,\n                registry: self.registry,\n                shutdown_rx: self.shutdown_rx,\n            },\n            in_tx,\n            out_rx,\n        )\n    }\n```\n\n### 3. Sentiment Analysis Stage (CPU-Intensive)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_27.txt\n// Lines: 302-327 (Naive sentiment strategy - CPU intensive)\npub struct NaiveSentiment;\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EnrichmentStrategy for NaiveSentiment {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"naive_sentiment\"\n    }\n\n    async fn enrich(&self, event: &SocialEvent) -> Result<Option<Enrichment>, StrategyError> {\n        // Simulate heavy computation.\n        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50)).await;\n\n        let score = if event.text.contains(\"love\") {\n            1.0\n        } else if event.text.contains(\"hate\") {\n            -1.0\n        } else {\n            0.0\n        };\n\n        let mut map = Enrichment::new();\n        map.insert(\"sentiment_score\".into(), serde_json::json!(score));\n        Ok(Some(map))\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 4. Back-pressure Mechanism in Current Architecture\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_78.txt\n// Lines: 362-365 (Back-pressure when channel is full)\n                    Ok(enriched) => {\n                        let latency = SystemTime::now().duration_since(started).unwrap_or_default();\n                        histogram!(\"pipeline.event.latency_ms\", latency.as_millis() as f64);\n                        if let Err(err) = output.send(enriched).await {\n                            error!(error=%err, \"failed to forward enriched event\");\n                            return Err(PipelineError::ChannelClosed);\n                        }\n                    }\n```\n\n### 5. Current Kafka Configuration\n\n```toml\n# File: /app/project/src/config.txt\n# Lines: 60-77 (Pipeline configuration showing worker pool sizes)\n[[pipelines]]\nname               = \"language_detection\"\nenabled            = true\ninput_topic        = \"social_raw\"\noutput_topic       = \"social_lang\"\nworker_pool_size   = 16\ntimeout_ms         = 3_000\nstrategy_impl      = \"lang_detect::FastTextStrategy\"\n\n[[pipelines]]\nname               = \"toxicity_scoring\"\nenabled            = true\ninput_topic        = \"social_lang\"\noutput_topic       = \"social_toxic\"\nworker_pool_size   = 24\ntimeout_ms         = 5_000\ndependencies       = [\"language_detection\"]\nstrategy_impl      = \"toxicity::PerspectiveStrategy\"\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### 1. Pipeline Mapping (4 Primary Stages)\n\nThe ChirpPulse data processing pipeline consists of the following stages:\n\n| Stage | Purpose | Key Module(s) |\n|-------|---------|---------------|\n| **1. Ingestion** | Consumes raw social events from external sources (Twitter, Reddit, Discord) via Kafka | module_12, module_47, module_70 |\n| **2. Data Normalization/Quality Control** | Schema validation, deduplication, PII removal, language detection | module_79, module_29, module_41, module_30 |\n| **3. Sentiment Analysis/Enrichment** | CPU-intensive sentiment scoring, toxicity detection, graph extraction | module_22, module_27, module_56, module_10 |\n| **4. Storage Sink** | Writes enriched data to Apache Iceberg tables on S3 | module_65, Kafka producer to `chirp.enriched.events` |\n\n### 2. Bottleneck Analysis: The tokio::sync::mpsc Channel Coupling\n\n**Identified Mechanism:** The coupling between the normalization stage and sentiment analysis stage occurs via `tokio::sync::mpsc::channel` with a bounded capacity.\n\n**Critical Code Location:** `/app/project/src/module_27.txt` lines 216-217\n\n```rust\nconst CHANNEL_DEPTH: usize = 1_024;\n// ...\nlet (in_tx, in_rx) = mpsc::channel(CHANNEL_DEPTH);\nlet (out_tx, out_rx) = mpsc::channel(CHANNEL_DEPTH);\n```\n\n**How This Causes Backpressure:**\n\n1. **Bounded Buffer Exhaustion**: The `mpsc::channel(1_024)` creates a bounded channel with capacity for only 1,024 events. When the sentiment analysis stage (consumer) processes events slower than the normalization stage (producer) produces them, the buffer fills up.\n\n2. **Blocking Send Behavior**: When the channel is full, `output.send(enriched).await` blocks the producer task, causing backpressure to propagate upstream to the normalization stage.\n\n3. **Single-Process Coupling**: Both producer and consumer run in the same process. If the sentiment analysis stage becomes overloaded (e.g., ML model inference latency spikes), it directly impacts the normalization stage's throughput.\n\n4. **No Durability**: In-memory mpsc channels provide no persistence. If the process crashes while the channel contains events, those events are lost.\n\n5. **Vertical Scaling Limitation**: The consumer and producer cannot be scaled independently - they are coupled in the same deployment unit.\n\n**With the FireHose Integration**: The new high-throughput source will produce events at a rate far exceeding the sentiment analysis stage's capacity. The bounded channel (1,024 events) will fill within milliseconds, causing:\n- Upstream stalling (normalization stops accepting new events)\n- Potential memory pressure from buffered events\n- Risk of data loss during process restarts\n\n### 3. Proposed Architectural Solution: External Message Queue Decoupling\n\n**Pattern: Asynchronous Message Queue / Event-Driven Architecture**\n\nReplace the in-process `tokio::sync::mpsc` channel with an external, durable message queue (Apache Kafka) to decouple the normalization and sentiment analysis stages.\n\n**Proposed Architecture:**\n\n```\n┌─────────────────┐      ┌──────────────────┐      ┌───────────────────┐\n│   Ingestion     │      │   Normalization  │      │    Sentiment      │\n│   Gateway       │ ──▶  │   Service        │ ──▶  │    Service        │\n│ (multiple pods) │      │ (multiple pods)  │      │ (auto-scaled)     │\n└─────────────────┘      └──────────────────┘      └───────────────────┘\n        │                         │                         │\n        │                         │                         │\n        ▼                         ▼                         ▼\n ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n │                        Apache Kafka Cluster                       │\n │  ┌────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────┐  ┌───────────────┐  │\n │  │ raw.events     │  │ normalized.events   │  │ enriched      │  │\n │  │ (partitioned)  │  │ (partitioned)       │  │ .events       │  │\n │  └────────────────┘  └─────────────────────┘  └───────────────┘  │\n └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n**Key Changes:**\n\n1. **Introduce `chirp.normalized.events` Kafka Topic**: Create a new intermediate Kafka topic between normalization and sentiment analysis.\n\n2. **Normalization Service as Kafka Producer**: After normalizing events, publish to `chirp.normalized.events` instead of an in-process channel.\n\n3. **Sentiment Service as Independent Kafka Consumer**: Run sentiment analysis as a separate microservice that consumes from `chirp.normalized.events` and produces to `chirp.enriched.events`.\n\n4. **Consumer Group Scaling**: The sentiment service can scale horizontally via Kafka consumer groups, with each consumer handling a subset of partitions.\n\n### 4. Justification of Proposed Solution\n\n| Benefit | Explanation |\n|---------|-------------|\n| **Scalability** | Sentiment analysis can scale independently by adding more consumer instances. Kafka partitions enable parallel processing across multiple pods. |\n| **Resilience** | Kafka provides message durability. If the sentiment service crashes, messages persist and can be reprocessed. No data loss during deployments or failures. |\n| **Backpressure Handling** | Kafka acts as an elastic buffer. The normalization stage continues producing at full speed while the sentiment stage processes at its own pace. Lag is absorbed by Kafka's disk-backed storage. |\n| **Decoupled Deployment** | Each stage can be deployed, updated, and scaled independently without affecting others. |\n| **Observability** | Consumer lag metrics from Kafka provide visibility into processing throughput and bottlenecks. |\n| **Exactly-Once Semantics** | Kafka's transactional semantics (already configured in config.txt: `enable_idempotence = true`) ensure no duplicate processing. |\n\n**Why This Pattern Over Alternatives:**\n\n- **vs. Increasing channel buffer**: Does not address fundamental scalability limits; still single-process.\n- **vs. Adding more worker threads**: Limited by single-machine resources; sentiment analysis is CPU/GPU bound.\n- **vs. Batch processing**: Loses real-time latency requirements stated in the README.\n\n### 5. Affected Components and Required Modifications\n\n| Module | File | Required Changes |\n|--------|------|------------------|\n| **module_27** | /app/project/src/module_27.txt | Replace `mpsc::channel` with Kafka producer. module_27 (Enrichment Orchestrator) - contains the critical mpsc channel coupling\n  - module_22 (Sentiment Stream Processor) - the slow consumer\n  - module_79 (QC Pipeline) - upstream producer\n  - Pipeline Stages Identified in Data Flow\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/constants.txt\n// Lines: 37-47 (Kafka topics showing pipeline stages)\npub mod kafka_topics {\n    /// Raw, unvalidated social events as produced by the ingress gateways.\n    pub const RAW_EVENTS: &str       = \"chirp.raw.events\";\n    /// Events that passed schema validation and deduplication.\n    pub const CLEAN_EVENTS: &str     = \"chirp.clean.events\";\n    /// Enriched events (e.g. lang detect, sentiment scores).\n    pub const ENRICHED_EVENTS: &str  = \"chirp.enriched.events\";\n    /// Dead-letter queue for poison pills.\n    pub const DLQ: &str              = \"chirp.dlq\";\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Critical Bottleneck: The mpsc Channel Coupling\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_27.txt\n// Lines: 44-47 (Channel depth constant)\n/// Maximum time a strategy may take before the orchestrator aborts it.\nconst STRATEGY_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_millis(800);\n\n/// Bounded size for the internal event queue.\nconst CHANNEL_DEPTH: usize = 1_024;\n```\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_27.txt\n// Lines: 209-228 (The critical bottleneck - bounded mpsc channels)\n    pub fn build(\n        self,\n    ) -> (\n        EnrichmentOrchestrator,\n        mpsc::Sender<SocialEvent>,\n        mpsc::Receiver<EnrichedEvent>,\n    ) {\n        let (in_tx, in_rx) = mpsc::channel(CHANNEL_DEPTH);\n        let (out_tx, out_rx) = mpsc::channel(CHANNEL_DEPTH);\n        (\n            EnrichmentOrchestrator {\n                input_rx: in_rx,\n                output_tx: out_tx,\n                registry: self.registry,\n                shutdown_rx: self.shutdown_rx,\n            },\n            in_tx,\n            out_rx,\n        )\n    }\n```\n\n### 3. Sentiment Analysis Stage (CPU-Intensive)\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_27.txt\n// Lines: 302-327 (Naive sentiment strategy - CPU intensive)\npub struct NaiveSentiment;\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl EnrichmentStrategy for NaiveSentiment {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"naive_sentiment\"\n    }\n\n    async fn enrich(&self, event: &SocialEvent) -> Result<Option<Enrichment>, StrategyError> {\n        // Simulate heavy computation.\n        tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(50)).await;\n\n        let score = if event.text.contains(\"love\") {\n            1.0\n        } else if event.text.contains(\"hate\") {\n            -1.0\n        } else {\n            0.0\n        };\n\n        let mut map = Enrichment::new();\n        map.insert(\"sentiment_score\".into(), serde_json::json!(score));\n        Ok(Some(map))\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 4. Back-pressure Mechanism in Current Architecture\n\n```rust\n// File: /app/project/src/module_78.txt\n// Lines: 362-365 (Back-pressure when channel is full)\n                    Ok(enriched) => {\n                        let latency = SystemTime::now().duration_since(started).unwrap_or_default();\n                        histogram!(\"pipeline.event.latency_ms\", latency.as_millis() as f64);\n                        if let Err(err) = output.send(enriched).await {\n                            error!(error=%err, \"failed to forward enriched event\");\n                            return Err(PipelineError::ChannelClosed);\n                        }\n                    }\n```\n\n### 5. Current Kafka Configuration\n\n```toml\n# File: /app/project/src/config.txt\n# Lines: 60-77 (Pipeline configuration showing worker pool sizes)\n[[pipelines]]\nname               = \"language_detection\"\nenabled            = true\ninput_topic        = \"social_raw\"\noutput_topic       = \"social_lang\"\nworker_pool_size   = 16\ntimeout_ms         = 3_000\nstrategy_impl      = \"lang_detect::FastTextStrategy\"\n\n[[pipelines]]\nname               = \"toxicity_scoring\"\nenabled            = true\ninput_topic        = \"social_lang\"\noutput_topic       = \"social_toxic\"\nworker_pool_size   = 24\ntimeout_ms         = 5_000\ndependencies       = [\"language_detection\"]\nstrategy_impl      = \"toxicity::PerspectiveStrategy\"\n```\n\n## Analysis\n\n### 1. Pipeline Mapping (4 Primary Stages)\n\nThe ChirpPulse data processing pipeline consists of the following stages:\n\n| Stage | Purpose | Key Module(s) |\n|-------|---------|---------------|\n| **1. Ingestion** | Consumes raw social events from external sources (Twitter, Reddit, Discord) via Kafka | module_12, module_47, module_70 |\n| **2. Data Normalization/Quality Control** | Schema validation, deduplication, PII removal, language detection | module_79, module_29, module_41, module_30 |\n| **3. Sentiment Analysis/Enrichment** | CPU-intensive sentiment scoring, toxicity detection, graph extraction | module_22, module_27, module_56, module_10 |\n| **4. Storage Sink** | Writes enriched data to Apache Iceberg tables on S3 | module_65, Kafka producer to `chirp.enriched.events` |\n\n### 2. Bottleneck Analysis: The tokio::sync::mpsc Channel Coupling\n\n**Identified Mechanism:** The coupling between the normalization stage and sentiment analysis stage occurs via `tokio::sync::mpsc::channel` with a bounded capacity.\n\n**Critical Code Location:** `/app/project/src/module_27.txt` lines 216-217\n\n```rust\nconst CHANNEL_DEPTH: usize = 1_024;\n// ...\nlet (in_tx, in_rx) = mpsc::channel(CHANNEL_DEPTH);\nlet (out_tx, out_rx) = mpsc::channel(CHANNEL_DEPTH);\n```\n\n**How This Causes Backpressure:**\n\n1. **Bounded Buffer Exhaustion**: The `mpsc::channel(1_024)` creates a bounded channel with capacity for only 1,024 events. When the sentiment analysis stage (consumer) processes events slower than the normalization stage (producer) produces them, the buffer fills up.\n\n2. **Blocking Send Behavior**: When the channel is full, `output.send(enriched).await` blocks the producer task, causing backpressure to propagate upstream to the normalization stage.\n\n3. **Single-Process Coupling**: Both producer and consumer run in the same process. If the sentiment analysis stage becomes overloaded (e.g., ML model inference latency spikes), it directly impacts the normalization stage's throughput.\n\n4. **No Durability**: In-memory mpsc channels provide no persistence. If the process crashes while the channel contains events, those events are lost.\n\n5. **Vertical Scaling Limitation**: The consumer and producer cannot be scaled independently - they are coupled in the same deployment unit.\n\n**With the FireHose Integration**: The new high-throughput source will produce events at a rate far exceeding the sentiment analysis stage's capacity. The bounded channel (1,024 events) will fill within milliseconds, causing:\n- Upstream stalling (normalization stops accepting new events)\n- Potential memory pressure from buffered events\n- Risk of data loss during process restarts\n\n### 3. Proposed Architectural Solution: External Message Queue Decoupling\n\n**Pattern: Asynchronous Message Queue / Event-Driven Architecture**\n\nReplace the in-process `tokio::sync::mpsc` channel with an external, durable message queue (Apache Kafka) to decouple the normalization and sentiment analysis stages.\n\n**Proposed Architecture:**\n\n```\n┌─────────────────┐      ┌──────────────────┐      ┌───────────────────┐\n│   Ingestion     │      │   Normalization  │      │    Sentiment      │\n│   Gateway       │ ──▶  │   Service        │ ──▶  │    Service        │\n│ (multiple pods) │      │ (multiple pods)  │      │ (auto-scaled)     │\n└─────────────────┘      └──────────────────┘      └───────────────────┘\n        │                         │                         │\n        │                         │                         │\n        ▼                         ▼                         ▼\n ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n │                        Apache Kafka Cluster                       │\n │  ┌────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────┐  ┌───────────────┐  │\n │  │ raw.events     │  │ normalized.events   │  │ enriched      │  │\n │  │ (partitioned)  │  │ (partitioned)       │  │ .events       │  │\n │  └────────────────┘  └─────────────────────┘  └───────────────┘  │\n └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n**Key Changes:**\n\n1. **Introduce `chirp.normalized.events` Kafka Topic**: Create a new intermediate Kafka topic between normalization and sentiment analysis.\n\n2. **Normalization Service as Kafka Producer**: After normalizing events, publish to `chirp.normalized.events` instead of an in-process channel.\n\n3. **Sentiment Service as Independent Kafka Consumer**: Run sentiment analysis as a separate microservice that consumes from `chirp.normalized.events` and produces to `chirp.enriched.events`.\n\n4. **Consumer Group Scaling**: The sentiment service can scale horizontally via Kafka consumer groups, with each consumer handling a subset of partitions.\n\n### 4. Justification of Proposed Solution\n\n| Benefit | Explanation |\n|---------|-------------|\n| **Scalability** | Sentiment analysis can scale independently by adding more consumer instances. Kafka partitions enable parallel processing across multiple pods. |\n| **Resilience** | Kafka provides message durability. If the sentiment service crashes, messages persist and can be reprocessed. No data loss during deployments or failures. |\n| **Backpressure Handling** | Kafka acts as an elastic buffer. The normalization stage continues producing at full speed while the sentiment stage processes at its own pace. Lag is absorbed by Kafka's disk-backed storage. |\n| **Decoupled Deployment** | Each stage can be deployed, updated, and scaled independently without affecting others. |\n| **Observability** | Consumer lag metrics from Kafka provide visibility into processing throughput and bottlenecks. |\n| **Exactly-Once Semantics** | Kafka's transactional semantics (already configured in config.txt: `enable_idempotence = true`) ensure no duplicate processing. |\n\n**Why This Pattern Over Alternatives:**\n\n- **vs. Increasing channel buffer**: Does not address fundamental scalability limits; still single-process.\n- **vs. Adding more worker threads**: Limited by single-machine resources; sentiment analysis is CPU/GPU bound.\n- **vs. Batch processing**: Loses real-time latency requirements stated in the README.\n\n### 5. Affected Components and Required Modifications\n\n| Module | File | Required Changes |\n|--------|------|------------------|\n| **module_27** | /app/project/src/module_27.txt | Replace `mpsc::channel` with Kafka producer. Change `build()` to return a Kafka topic name instead of `mpsc::Sender`. Modify `run()` loop to produce to Kafka instead of channel. |\n| **module_22** | /app/project/src/module_22.txt | Already uses Kafka. Update `INPUT_TOPIC` constant to consume from the new intermediate topic `chirp.normalized.events`. Ensure consumer group configuration supports horizontal scaling. |\n| **module_56** | /app/project/src/module_56.txt | Replace `mpsc::Receiver<api::RawEvent>` with Kafka consumer. Modify `SentimentEngine::new()` to create a Kafka consumer instead of receiving an mpsc channel. |\n| **module_79** | /app/project/src/module_79.txt | Update `SINK_TOPIC` to point to the new intermediate topic. Ensure producer is configured for high throughput. |\n| **config.txt** | /app/project/src/config.txt | Add new `[[pipelines]]` entry for the intermediate topic. Add new Kafka topic to `kafka_topics` section. |\n| **constants.txt** | /app/project/src/constants.txt | Add `pub const NORMALIZED_EVENTS: &str = \"chirp.normalized.events\";` to the `kafka_topics` module. |\n\n**Specific Code Changes for module_27:**\n\n```rust\n// BEFORE (module_27.txt, lines 216-217):\nlet (in_tx, in_rx) = mpsc::channel(CHANNEL_DEPTH);\nlet (out_tx, out_rx) = mpsc::channel(CHANNEL_DEPTH);\n\n// AFTER:\n// Remove in-process channels entirely.\n// Normalization publishes directly to Kafka topic.\n// Sentiment service consumes from Kafka topic in separate process.\n```\n\n**Specific Config Changes for config.txt:**\n\n```toml\n# Add new intermediate topic configuration\n[[pipelines]]\nname               = \"normalization_output\"\nenabled            = true\ninput_topic        = \"social_qc_valid\"\noutput_topic       = \"social_normalized\"  # NEW INTERMEDIATE TOPIC\nworker_pool_size   = 16\ntimeout_ms         = 2_000\n\n[[pipelines]]\nname               = \"sentiment_analysis\"\nenabled            = true\ninput_topic        = \"social_normalized\"  # CONSUME FROM NEW TOPIC\noutput_topic       = \"social_enriched\"\nworker_pool_size   = 32  # Can be increased independently\ntimeout_ms         = 10_000\nauto_scale         = true  # Enable Kubernetes HPA\n```\n\n## Summary\n\n- **Primary finding**: The bottleneck between normalization and sentiment analysis is caused by a bounded `tokio::sync::mpsc::channel(1_024)` in module_27, which creates tight coupling and backpressure when the sentiment stage cannot keep pace with incoming events.\n\n- **Key components**:\n  - Here's a summary of my findings:\n\n## Analysis Complete\n\n### 1. Pipeline Mapping (4 Primary Stages)\n- **Ingestion**: Consumes raw events from social sources via Kafka (modules 12, 47, 70)\n- **Normalization/QC**: Schema validation, deduplication, PII removal (modules 79, 29, 41)\n- **Sentiment Analysis/Enrichment**: CPU-intensive scoring and toxicity detection (modules 22, 27, 56)\n- **Storage Sink**: Writes to Apache Iceberg via Kafka (module 65)\n\n### 2. Bottleneck Identified\nThe critical coupling is in **module_27.txt** at lines 216-217:\n```rust\nconst CHANNEL_DEPTH: usize = 1_024;\nlet (in_tx, in_rx) = mpsc::channel(CHANNEL_DEPTH);\nlet (out_tx, out_rx) = mpsc::channel(CHANNEL_DEPTH);\n```\n\nThis bounded `tokio::sync::mpsc` channel creates tight coupling between normalization and sentiment analysis. When the sentiment stage (slow consumer) can't keep pace, the 1,024-event buffer fills, blocking the producer and propagating backpressure upstream.\n\n### 3. Proposed Solution: Message Queue Decoupling\nReplace the in-process mpsc channel with an external **Apache Kafka topic** (`chirp.normalized.events`) to decouple the stages. This enables:\n- Independent horizontal scaling of sentiment consumers\n- Durable buffering during load spikes\n- Elastic backpressure absorption\n- Fault tolerance\n\n### 4. Affected Modules\n- **module_27**: Replace mpsc with Kafka producer\n- **module_22**: Update to consume from new intermediate topic\n- **module_56**: Replace mpsc receiver with Kafka consumer\n- **config.txt**: Add new pipeline configuration\n- **constants.txt**: Add new topic constant\n\nThe complete solution with code evidence and detailed implementation guidance has been written to `/logs/agent/solution.md`.","session_id":"4fdf0dd4-72e6-406f-a1e5-abf3ce41b3be","total_cost_usd":1.8164410000000002,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":97474,"cache_read_input_tokens":1946679,"output_tokens":9112,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":97474}},"modelUsage":{"claude-opus-4-5-20251101":{"inputTokens":2,"outputTokens":9112,"cacheReadInputTokens":1946679,"cacheCreationInputTokens":97474,"webSearchRequests":0,"costUSD":1.810362,"contextWindow":200000,"maxOutputTokens":64000},"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001":{"inputTokens":5279,"outputTokens":160,"cacheReadInputTokens":0,"cacheCreationInputTokens":0,"webSearchRequests":0,"costUSD":0.006079,"contextWindow":200000,"maxOutputTokens":64000}},"permission_denials":[],"uuid":"770dd941-7359-4e45-abc9-18b1721400ba"}
